public hearing for the 2015‐2016 proposed budget.  Mr. West 
presented  the  details  of  the  proposed  budget  to  the  Board 
shared that the proposed budget was reviewed in great detail 
with  the  Board  at  the  June  16th  workshop.   He  talked  about 
tax  values  and  homestead/non‐homestead  forecasts.   He 
talked  about  the  foundation  allowance  and  the  increase  in 
student enrollment.   He also reviewed general fund revenues 
and expenditures.  
Infrastructure  Grant)  grant  and  how  it  helped  the  District  in

and  its  closing  so  that  the  public  would  have  a  better 
understanding  of  it.   Mr.  West  clarified  the  Durant  Fund 
settlement  and  why  the  funds  were  being  eliminated.   Dr. 
Philippart  commented  on  the  benefits  of  holding  a  public 
hearing  on  the  budget  so  that  the  audience  can  understand 
the criticality of why the District must maintain a fund balance 
of 10 – 12% for cash flow purposes.  Mr. West mentioned that 
MSBO  (Michigan  School  Business  Officials)  recommends  that 
districts  try  to  maintain  a  15%  fund  balance  to  avoid  costs 
related to borrowing for general operating purposes.   
Dr.  Philippart  invited  members  of  the  audience  to  ask 
questions or share comments.

from  all  four  high  schools  were  chosen  as  the  inaugural 
members  of  the  Superintendent’s  Student  Advisory  Board. 
This  group  worked  together  to  develop  a  mission  statement 
students.  They  worked  diligently  and  collaboratively  all  year 
to  lend  a  student  perspective  on  important  issues  and 
provided  insight  on  the  student  experience.   Though  our 
graduating seniors on the Board will move on to colleges and 
universities  this  fall,  we  are  hoping  to  see  our  sophomores, 
juniors  and  new  seniors  back  on  the  Board  next  year  when 
they  begin  to  put  some  of  their  plans  into  action.   At  this 
evening’s  meeting  Dr.  Machesky  and  the  Board  of  Education 
were  pleased  to  recognize  the  following  students  for  their 
service, their willingness to give of their time and talents and 
their dedication to the goals they created.

Education are pleased to honor Mr. Raad Alawan and Mr. Ed 
Morykwas for their hard work and commitment to spreading 
the good news of the Troy School District through School Life 
Troy. The District entered into a partnership with School Life 
this  year  and  the  feedback  from  parents,  staff  and  students 
has  been  very  positive.  Each  month,  our  students  and  staff 
are  showcased  for  their  achievements,  charitable  endeavors 
and  activities  inside  and  outside  school.  Mr.  Alawan  is  the 
publisher of School Life Troy and spends a great deal of time 
in  the  District  to  gather  content  and  ensure  the  smooth 
running of this newspaper. Mr. Morykwas is the photographer 
for  School  Life  and  had  captured  and  memorialized  many 
wonderful moments this past school year. From the first issue 
last September to the graduation issue a couple of weeks ago, 
School Life Troy has been a wonderful addition to the District 
and  one  that  we  hope  is  here  for  many  years  to  come.   Mr. 
Alawan and Mr. Morykwas were honored with a certificate of 
recognition  and  invited  to  say  a  few  words.   Mr.  Alawan 
intorduced  the  team  at  School  Life.   He  then  thanked  Ms. 
Birmingham who spent much time working with him this past 
year along with the District administration and secretaries for 
their  assistance.   He  thanked  the  School  Life  staff  and  his 
team for working so hard to get the papers out on time and 
into  the  hands  of  the  students  and  staff.   Dr.  Philippart  
thanked  the  School  Life  team  and  shared  that  School  Life  is 
provided  to  the  District  at  zero‐cost  due  to  the  advertising 
and support and of the Troy community.

It  was  moved  by  Mr.  Schmidt  and  supported  by  Ms. 
Underwood that  
1954, as amended (the “Michigan Election Law”); 
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  will  hold  a  Special 
School  Election  on  November  3,  2015,  which  Special  School 
Election  will  be  conducted  by  the  Clerk  of  the  County  of 
Oakland;  
  WHEREAS, the Michigan Election Law requires that the 
School  District  certify  the  ballot  language  for  any  proposals  to 
be voted on at a permitted election date to the School District’s 
Election  Coordinator  not  later  than  4:00  p.m.,  on  the  twelfth 
Tuesday before election date; 
  WHEREAS,  Public  Act  336  of  1993  amended  the  State 
School  Aid  Act  by  guaranteeing  to  each  local  school  district  a 
base “foundation allowance” per membership pupil;  
  WHEREAS,  in  order  to  receive  the  full  guaranteed 
foundation allowance per membership pupil, the School District 
is  required  to  levy  millage  locally  on  all  property,  except  
property exempted by law; 
  WHEREAS, the previously authorized operating millages 
of the School District are set to expire on December 31, 2015; 
  WHEREAS, the School District has determined that it will 
be  necessary  to  continue  the  operating  millage  authorizations 
to provide for the operating needs of the School District; 
  WHEREAS, the School District also desires to request an 
increase to the operating millage authorization assessed against 
all  property,  except  property  exempted  by  law,  in  order  to 
restore  the  portion  of  the  operating  millage  lost  as  a  result  of 
the  reduction  required  by  the  Michigan  Constitution  (i.e.,  the 
Headlee rollback) and to protect against future reductions. 
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  desires  to  submit  a 
proposal  to  approve  the  continuation  and  increase  of  the 
operating millage authorizations at a Special School Election to 
be held on November 3, 2015; and 
  WHEREAS,  the  School  District  desires  to  approve  the 
above  referenced  operating  millage  proposal  and  to  authorize 
the  Superintendent  or  his  designee  to  certify  the  ballot 
language  for  the  operating  millage  proposal  to  the  School 
District’s Election Coordinator for the Special School Election to 
be held on November 3, 2015. 
  7 June 23, 2015

  NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE BOARD OF

hereto as Exhibit A, is hereby approved.   
  2.  The Superintendent or his designee is authorized 
to certify the above referenced Proposal to the School District’s 
Election Coordinator on or before August 11, 2015. 
  3.  The Superintendent or his designee is furthered 
authorized  to  take  any  and  all  action  required  under  the 
Michigan  Election  Law  with  regard  to  the  School  District’s 
Special School Election. 
  4.  All  resolutions  and  parts  of  resolutions  insofar 
as they conflict with the provisions of this resolution are hereby 
rescinded.

thanked  everyone  for  a  wonderful  school  year  and  wished

6. B.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
     Supervisor, Special Education  
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. Adrian Dean to the 
position of Supervisor, Special Education.  Mr. Dean comes to Troy from the 
Waterford School District where he spent the past 7 years serving as first a 
behavioral consultant and later as principal at Waterford’s Kingsley Montgomery 
School which serves as one of the Oakland ISD’s center -based programs.  Mr. 
Dean rec eived his Education Specialist degree from Wayne State University in 
2009 focusing on Autism Spectrum Disorders.  He holds his Master  of Arts and

I believe there many skills and attributes that are necessary to be an outstanding teacher. Some of the most important qualities are 
passion, leadership, classroom management and content knowledge. A teacher has many roles throughout the school day, therefore 
requires various qualities to be an outstanding educator. Throughout the school day, teachers accommodate the academic, social, and 
emotional needs of all of their students.

resourceful and productive. An effective teacher engages all students into each lesson so they feel like they are part of the classroom 
community. Thus, when a teacher engages all students then the students become more active in the learning process. Furthermore, an 
outstanding teacher is extremely knowledgeable and understanding of the content being presented. It is important that teach teacher

Statement
ADM1. What personal characteristics define an excellent administrator?
To begin, as a foundation, school administrators must have strong content knowledge in public school law, finance, K-12 curriculum, and 
professional development needs. However, this foundation only scratches the surface of the skillset of the successful school leader. 
Administrators need to have irrepressible conflict resolution skills, interpersonal skills, organizational skills, and strong communication 
abilities. Furthermore, the successful school leader understands the importance of shared leadership, empowering employees, encourages 
innovation, and the importance of building a vibrant school climate and culture. Finally, a strong leader is an individual who is 
continuously trying to improve his or her practice through expanding their skill-set and knowledge.

students. Finally, there are many intricate federal and state legalities when it comes to supporting students with disabilities. Having a

To Whom It May Concern:
It is with great excitement that I apply for an educational leadership position with school 
districts within Oakland County!  With over 15 years of diverse experiences in the K-12 
education profession, I feel I will be a valuable asset and strong educational leader. 
Most recently, as Principal of Kingsley Montgomery School, I have provided leadership to 
one of Oakland County’s largest “programs for all”.  Within this current position, it is most 
important to me to ensure that Oakland County students with disabilities have access to 
specialized programming that meet their unique, and often, complex needs.  This experience 
has allowed me to gain countless valuable skills, including, leading over 70 employees, 
conflict resolution, collaborating with families, facilitating small and large groups in 
professional development, problem solving within a systematic team approach, and leading 
staff in designing specialized education interventions and programs for some of Oakland 
County’s most intricate students. 
Previous to my position as principal, I provided consultative services on behalf of students 
with autism spectrum disorder, and behavioral challenges.  Within this role I collaborated 
with staff on the implementation of evidence based approaches for students with ASD, 
positive behavioral interventions and supports, functional behavior assessments, and 
maximizing inclusive opportunities to ensure students reach their full education potential. 
Furthermore this opportunity allowed me to advise district administration on the redesign 
of the school districts pre-k through postsecondary ASD programs and service delivery.  
In addition to my extensive professional background, you will see that I have had numerous 
respected education and professional development experiences. Beyond my education 
specialist degree, I am currently perusing administrative certifications in the following 
areas; K-12 administration, special education and central office. Additionally, as outlined on 
my resume, I have attended numerous professional development opportunities that have 
strengthened my leadership skills.  I believe my current skillset and knowledge are 
expansive, yet I continue to grow and develop to meet the ever evolving challenges of public 
education.   
It is my combination of passion for student success, leadership experience and my 
dedication to being a lifelong learner that I feel will greatly benefit school districts.  I am 
excited to discuss how I can contribute in leading your school district’s vision and mission 
in greater detail with you.  Please feel free to contact me if you require additional 
information in considering me as an interview candidate.

Re: Recommendation for Adrian Dean
I am pleased to recommend Adrian Dean for a position as a Special Education Administrator.  I 
have worked with Adrian in the Waterford School District in his roles as both a consultant for 
students with Autism and as Supervisor of the Kingsley Montgomery School.
Adrian has classroom and consultant experience working with students with a variety of 
challenges including Autism and Emotional Impairments.  Adrian made a successful transition to 
his current administrative role, replacing me as administrator at Kingsley Montgomery School 
(KMS).  Adrian was at KMS the previous year in a consultant capacity working intimately with 
the various programs at the school.  In his new capacity, he quickly learned the many aspects of 
the position as an administrator.  Adrian has a confident approach to his work and shows sound 
judgment by asking for input from stakeholders regarding how best to address challenging and 
complex situations.  He is able to lead others to reach collaborative decisions, while employing 
the resources necessary to guide sound decision making.     
Adrian is self-motivated and fulfills his job expectations with little to no supervision.  He readily 
develops professional relationships within the school district and in the community of special 
educators.  Adrian is well respected by those staff whom he supervises.  He has brought 
positive changes to the programs at KMS and holds his staff to high expectations.  Adrian is a 
lifelong learner who seeks out opportunities to develop professionally through professional 
organizations, workshops and the relationships he has worked to build with other programs. 
It has been my pleasure to work with Adrian.  I am confident you will find him to be an excellent 
candidate for this position.   I am available to respond to any specific questions you may have 
and can be reached at 248-494-9027.
Sincerely,
John H. Gregory, M Ed.

Professional Profile
Currently, as Principal of Kingsley Montgomery School I am a dedicated and highly motivated educational 
administrative professional with a proven track record for leading and implementing successful research-based 
programs.  My areas of high expertise include PBIS, differentiated instruction, data analysis, adult 
learning/professional development, school climate/culture, special education services, and athletic 
administration. Alongside a goal oriented staff and administration, I look forward to supporting educational 
needs of all students.

A separate grading system is used for students in the graduate-
professional (Juris Doctor) program.
The honor point average (gpa) is computed by multiplying the
honor points assigned to each course grade by the number of credit
hours for each course, adding the results and dividing by the total
number of credit hours.
                              A grade point average of 2.00 is required for
graduation.
                     A grade point average of 3.00 is required for graduation.
This grade point system became effective in Fall 1959.

A separate grading system is used for students in the graduate-
professional (Juris Doctor) program.
The honor point average (gpa) is computed by multiplying the
honor points assigned to each course grade by the number of credit
hours for each course, adding the results and dividing by the total
number of credit hours.
                              A grade point average of 2.00 is required for
graduation.
                     A grade point average of 3.00 is required for graduation.
This grade point system became effective in Fall 1959.

7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
     9th Grade for 2016 – 2017  
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  It 
is anticipated that seats will be available for the 2016-2017 school year for grade 
9 at the International Academy East.  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105c resolution for 90 non-resident 
9th grade students who attend a district in another county, if that intermediate 
school district is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District 
boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of the State School Aid 
Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a 
Schools of Choice program for the 2016-2017 school year for 90 non-resident 9th 
grade students who attend a school district in another county if that 
Intermediate School District is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School 
District boundaries for the purposes of attending the International Academy East 
program located in the Troy School District. 
Yes: _________ 
No: __________

8. BUSINESS  
 B.  CONSIDERATION:  VACANT PROPERTY CONSULTING 
SERVICES 
 In an effort to receive valuation of District owned vacant property, 
Administration is recommending a Pre -Sale Investigation of five (5) 
vacant lands be performed by Great Northern Consulting Group as 
outlined in their comprehensive proposal submitted July 29, 2015.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education authorize 
administration to accept Phase I of the proposal from  Great Northern 
Consulting Group and to proceed with Pre-Sale Investigation of  the five 
(5) District owned  vacant properties as listed in the proposal for  an 
amount not to exceed $17,500.00 and that this amount be approved as 
an expenditure of the Capital Maintenance Fund. 
 Yes: 
 No:

GREAT NORTHERN CONSULTING GROUP

Rick West 
Assistant Superintendent, Business Services 
Troy School District

Re: Pre-Sale Investigation/ Due Diligence/ Marketing  5 sites in Troy MI 
1. Section 1 Square Lake and John R 
2. 18 acres north of Long lake between Rochester and John R 
3. 15 and 20 acres North of Wattles East of Livernois 
4. 19.5 acres East of I-75 
5. 18 acres North of Big Beaver and west of John R 
Dear Mr. West: 
Please find attached our proposal for the pre-sale investigation/ due diligence and 
marketing for the 5 sites listed above. 
I. Scope Of The Project 
II. Compensation 
I.  SCOPE OF THE PROJECT 
Great Northern Consulting Group shall provide the following services on behalf of 
The Troy School District. 
 Phase I: PRE SALE INVESTIGATION   
· Determine existing zoning and potential upgrades to zoning 
· Review existing appraisals 
· Determine availability, location and capacity of water, storm sewer, and sani-
tary sewer.  
· Order Title Work or review existing Title Work and attachments to deter-
mine if any issues need to be resolved 
· Determine approval process and work necessary for approval process for re-
use or re-development. 
· Determine if any development incentives are available from City 
186
· Prepare budget for proposed planning and due diligence items with recom-
mendations 
· Determine highest and best use for the site 
· Determine Estimated Market Values  
· Review Existing Surveys if available 
· Review Existing environmental information if available 
· Prepare report with recommendations and budget for due diligence and 
marketing 
PHASE II:  PLANNING AND DUE DILIGENCE 
· Complete approved due diligence 
· Prepare report on results of Due Diligence and recommendations for Mar-
keting 
PHASE III: MARKETING    
· Determine method of Sale/Lease RFP   
· Create marketing plan/ mailing list  
· Determine deadlines if any 
· Create marketing package and duplicate for distribution 
· Create e-package for distribution 
· Post RFP Package on District Web Site  
· Distribution of marketing packages / via hard copies, CD-ROMs, and e-
packages  
· Prepare decision Matrix 
· Make recommendations to District 
*PHASE IV:  HOURLY CONSULTING  
· After initial marketing effort Great Northern will provide the following ser-
vices on an hourly basis approved by District.  
· Meetings with Qualified Purchasers  
· Assist Purchasers with Municipal approvals if necessary 
· Assist in negotiations  
· Attend Board meetings 
II. COMPENSATION: 
Great Northern Consulting Group will provide the services listed above on an hour-
ly fee basis. Troy School District will be billed hourly on a monthly basis at a rate of 
$185.00 per hour for Principal time and $125.00 per hour for support personnel. 
The monthly fee does not include engineering, Title Work, Environmental studies, 
advertisements, or printing and postage costs. However the scope of such work is at 
the discretion of the School District. The School District must approve any and all of 
these costs in advance in writing. These items will be added to the monthly invoice. 
Payment for monthly billing shall be due within 30 days of receipt of invoice. 
187
FEE FOR EACH PHASE: 
.   
· Phase I:  Pre Sale Investigation:   Pre-Sale Investigation will be completed 
for a not to exceed amount of  $17,500.00 ( If individual sites $5,000 per 
property) 
· Phase II: Due Diligence: After Pre- Sale Investigation is completed Great 
Northern Consulting Group will prepare a detailed budget for recommended 
due diligence Items including Great Northern Consulting hours required. 
This must be approved by the District in writing. This fee Great Northern 
Time) is estimated to be $4,000.00 ( if individual sites $1,500 per property)  
· Phase III: Marketing:  If approved by District after due diligence completed 
Great Northern Consulting Group will Complete Items Outlined in Phase III 
of this proposal. This work cost shall not exceed $ 60,000.00. ( if individual 
sites 15,000 per site) 
· Phase IV: Hourly Consulting: After initial marketing effort Great Northern 
will perform services outlined in Phase IV as approved by the District on an 
hourly basis. This Fee Will not exceed $12,000 ( if individual sites $5,000 per 
property).  
· Please note the service menu may change after pre-sale investigation com-
pleted or due diligence completed. The District may elect to select only cer-
tain phases of this proposal.  
The marketing packages will reflect the fact that the School District will not pay any 
commission. Brokers must look to their purchaser for any commission. Additionally 
the School District may cancel this agreement with 30 days notice at any time, for 
any reason or no reason, with or without cause.

 10. B.       08.04.15 
10. OTHER 
 B.  FIRST READING:   Board Policy J-3306 Epinephrine Auto- 
        Injectors (Epi-Pens)  
 Michigan’s Public Acts 186 and 187 requir e that each Michigan public and 
nonpublic school have at least two epinephrine auto -injectors, commonly 
known as “Epi-Pens,” in each school building.  Board of Education members 
have read and reviewed the new Policy J-3306, Epinephrine Auto -Injectors 
(Epi-Pens). The Board of Education’s legal counsel has also reviewed this 
policy a long with MCL 380.1178,  MCL 380.1178a and MCL 380.1179  
pertaining to the possession and use of Epinephrine Auto -Injectors and has 
determined that the policy as drafted conforms to the relevant statues of  
State law, the Open Meetings Act, and the Revised School Code. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Indicate that all Board members will be asked to 
review this policy for a second time prior to the next Board meeting and will vote on 
adoption of revisions under the consent agenda at the September Board meeting.   
192
STUDENT HEALTH     J-3300 
J-3306   EPINEPHRINE AUTO-INJECTORS (EPI-PENS) 
1. A stud ent m ay possess an epinephrine auto -injector (“epi-
pen”) at  a school building , on District-sponsored 
transportation, or at any activity, event, or  program 
sponsored by the District or  in wh ich the  student’s school 
building is participating, provided that: 
a. The stu dent has written approval to possess and use 
the epi-pen from the  student’s physician or other 
health care provider authorized by law to prescribe an 
epi-pen, and, if the student is  a minor, from the 
student's parent/legal guardian. 
b. T h e Principal or Principal’s Designee of the student’s 
school building  has rece ived a copy of each written 
approval required under subdivision (a) for the 
student. 
c. There is  on file at the stud ent's school building  a 
written emergency care plan that contains specific 
instructions for the student's needs, that is prepared by 
a physician licensed in this state in collaboration with  
the  student  and  the  student's  parent/legal  guardian,  
and  that  is upd ated as n ecessary for changing 
circumstances. 
2. In each school building  operated by the District with an 
instructional and administrative st aff of at least ten, there 
shall be at least two (2) employees at the school building who 
have been trained in the appropriate use and administration 
of an epi-pen.  In each school building  operated by t he 
District with an instructional and administrative staff of fewer 
than ten, there s hall be at l east one employee at the school 
building who has been trained in the appropriate use and 
administration of an epi-pen.  The training required under 
1 
193
this subp aragraph shall be conducted under t he sup ervision 
of, and shall include evaluation by, a  licensed registered 
professional nurse. 
3. The  District  shall  acquire  or  purchase  and  maintain  at  
least  two  (2) un expired epi-pens for and at each school 
building operated by the District. 
4. A licensed registered professional nurse who is employed or 
contracted by the Dist rict or a Dist rict employee who  is 
trained in the administration of an epi- pen under 
subparagraph (2), above, is authorized to administer an epi-
pen to a student who h as a prescription on file at the school 
building. 
5. A licensed registered professional nurse who is employed or 
contracted by the  District or a Dist rict employee who  is 
trained in the administration of an epi- pen under 
subparagraph (2), above, is authorized to  administer an epi-
pen on the grounds of a school building  to any oth er 
individual who is believed to be h aving an anaphylactic 
reaction. 
6. The District will notify the parent(s)/legal guardian  of any 
student to whom an epi-pen has b een administered by a 
District employee or contractor as soon as possible. 
7. This policy is not intended, and should not be construed, to 
create or grant any rights or remedies to any person.  This 
policy is drafted to comply with applicable law, and shall be 
interpreted and applied in a m anner to  be consistent with  
applicable l aw.  In the event that through either 
administrative, judicial or legislative action the law is clarified 
or changed such that any p rovision of this poli cy is  
inconsistent with the law by imposing requirements that are 
less than or exceed the law, then such provision shall no 
longer be applicable, and instead the requirements of the law 
as clarified or changed shall be followed. 
2 
194
8. The Superintendent shall establish administrative rules to 
implement this poli cy consistent with the requirements of 
2013 PA 187 and the Michigan Department of  Education’s 
Medication Administration Guidelines.
